Reduced Self-Efficacy: Distrust in one's own ability to perform tasks or solve problems.\n  - Chronic Sense of Inferiority: Persistently evaluating oneself lower in comparison to others.\n  - Lack of Self-Assurance: Continually feeling anxious and unsure about decisions or actions.\n  - Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I've just been feeling really down and sad for a very long time now. It has been going on constantly for about two years and two months, and I just can't seem to shake this depressed mood.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling down for about two years and two months. Have you experienced any periods where your mood significantly improved for at least two months during this time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall any times where my mood actually improved or felt normal during these past two years. It has just been a constant, heavy cloud without any real breaks.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you lost interest in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy, or have you still been able to find pleasure in the things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. It's more just that my mood itself is always so low.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by your mood being 'always so low'? Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).
